PREFACE 


Tas, the first European edition of the Vibhayga, is the 
work of Miss Chalmers, sister of Mr. Robert Chalmers, 
C.B., who transcribed it from the Siamese printed edition 
of the Buddhist canonical books. She completed the 
tedious and ungrateful task by the beginning of 1908. 
The further work of collation and publication was handed 
over to me during the following summer, and was begun 
in September, 1903. I have compared the Siamese, word 
for word, with the Sinhalese of a palm-leaf MS. obtained 
by Professor Rhys Davids in 1884 from the Vanavasa 
Vihāra in Ceylon. A copy of the Burmese printed edition 
of the Vibhanga reached me only as the present text was 
being printed. I have collated all differences of reading in 
the Sinhalese and Siamese with the Burmese. 

As to choice of reading in cases of discrepancy, I could 
wish that the present edition made a better show of con- 
sistency than it does. One gravitates naturally toward the 
traditions of Ceylon, as being the most venerable. My 
Sinhalese MS. proved, however, to be full of inconsistencies 
as well as slips of the stylus to an extent which might have 
congested my footnotes far more than they reveal. Hence 
I have only followed the Sinhalese outright where it has 
consistently followed the tradition of its schools—viz., in 
the vy of such words as avyākata, vyapada, vya- 
sana, dc., where Burma and Siam write by-; in 
upekha, sekha, vimokha, instead of the -kkha of 
both the latter; in the niggahita before "e" instead of 
the doubled ‘ii,’ and so on. Where it shows frequent in- 
consistency I have followed usually the Burmese and its 

xi 


xii Preface 


younger sister, the Siamese—e.g., in maha (Sinh. often 
maha), photthabba (Sinh. often potthabba), 
in many uses of the cerebral ‘n,’ often rejected by the 
Sinh., especially in panita, tanha, tini, and of the 
cerebral “|,” as in salayatana, and in the preference 
of ‘u’ to ‘u’ in the plural, e.g., in hetu. Here and 
there, it should be confessed, the Siamese has been 
suffered to stand, less because the reading specially 
commended itself than because it was first in the field 
for purposes of this present edition. And at this early 
stage of Pali scholarship it is scarcely legitimate to speak 
of authority in readings. 

I have again ventured, as in the previous volume issued 
by the Pali Text Society, to use the tailed ‘yn’ for nig- 
gahīta. Its lakkhana is unmistakable, even if it 
does not commend itself for beauty ; and to expect anyone 
who has experienced the boon of it to turn back to the 
cumbrous ‘m’ and ‘n’ is not reasonable. 

For the tabulation of terms and phrases in the Matika’s 
and elsewhere I am alone responsible. The sole idea has 
been, of course, to economize time and effort for the reader. 
But in all elisions (peyyala’s) I have simply followed 
one or more of the texts collated, all of which elide fre- 
quently, though not with mutual uniformity. 

It will be noticed that very many of these excisions are 
given, not only on occasion of repetitions in the answers, 
but also at their earliest occurrence. See, for instance, on 
pp. 2, 8 the enumeration of rupan sukhumar and 
rüpar santike, on pp. 71, 72—in the Siamese edition, 
at all events — the description of the āyatanāni, on 
pp. 106, 107 that of some of the maggaņgāni, and 
more especial on pp. 178, 179, where the dhammā 
forming the content of a citta, good or bad, are elided 
except the first and last terms (phasso...pe... 
avikkhepo), although, by reason of the ... pe... 
we cannot but infer that we may take as read the lists 
given in the opening replies of the Dhamma-Sangani, and 
repeated later on in that work, in connections similar to 


Preface , xili 


those in which they occur in the cited passages of the 
Vibhanga (cf., e.g., Dh. S., $$ 499 joli). In other words, 
the present book seems by Buddhists to have ranked second 
in the seven of its Pitaka not accidentally, but as a sequel 
to the Dhamma-Sangani, requiring, in those who came 
to the study of it, a familiarity with the categories and 
formulas of the latter work—that is, with the first book 
of the Abhidhamma. In fact, the Buddhist student who 
had achieved proficiency in the methods and phraseology 
of the Dhamma-Sangani would find himself already half- 
way towards proficiency in the Vibhanga at the first recita- 
tion of it. There is, practically speaking, no formula or 
category in the first book that is not taken up and applied 
in the second, with the exception of the meditations 
on Asubha, Appanihita, Animitta, The Four Magga’s, 
and the Twenty Mahānaya's. Even the summarizing 
appendix to the Dhamma-Sangani finds once an echo, 
fortunately very brief, in the summary of the Four 
Patisambhida ending the Abhidhammabhājaniya, on p. 308 
of the present work. The diction in these few lines is not 
on all fours with that of the context, either here or in the 
other vibhangas, but is identical with that in the Appendix 
alluded to, and the paragraph is probably an interpolation. 
On the other hand, the older and possibly contemporary 
fragment of Atthakatha ending the Nikkhepakanda of the 
Dhamma-Saygaņi finds, in our book, a much more expanded 
and integral parallel—a somewhat similar commentary 
forming a large proportion of five of the vibhangas. 

The extent to which the Vibhanga supplements the 
remarkable contribution to Buddhist psychology yielded 
by the Dhamma-Sangani is disappointingly slight. The 
compiler or compilers make but little use of the fine 
opportunity afforded by the subject of the first vibhanga. 
There is in that division nothing to compare with the 
analysis of rupa in the prior work; scarcely any affirma- 
tion of independent value, save, perhaps, the pregnant 
Ekavidhena's of the several skandhas, where the 
sayņkhārakkhandha alone is said to be citta- 


xiv Preface 


sampayutta, not phassasampayutta.! We 
have then to pass to the Nanavatthu-vibhanga before we 
come to some generalizations of fresh psychological interest 
on the five modes of sense-consciousness. 

But although the Vibhanga is so largely anticipated by 
the Dhamma-Sangani, it is by no means covered by the 
latter work, either in method or in matter. It contains, in 
the first place, a great number of terms and many defini- 
tions which find no place in the prior compilation. To 
name only a few that are of intrinsic interest, may a, 
as ‘deception’ is graphically described (p. 357), on ethical 
ground only, absolutely without metaphysical implications 
—this single view of the term holding good, I believe, for 
the whole of the Tipitaka. 

Again, we find, on p. 249, the words bod hipakkhiya 
(or, in the Siamese, °ka) dhamma. The term occurs 
already, as we know, in the Sanyutta Nikaya (v, 827, and 
337-9). But whereas it is there applied to the Five 
Indriyas—faith, energy, mindfulness, concentration, and 
wisdom—it here refers to the Seven Bojjhanga’s—mind- 
fulness, investigation (of dhamm a), energy, joy, serenity, 
concentration, equanimity. Both of the groups are, in the 
Sanyutta, said to conduce to bodha—bodhaya san- 
vattanti—but only the former are explicitly classed as 
bodha- or bodhi-pakkhiya (or -ka) dhamma. 
There is no indication, either there, or, so far as we know, 
elsewhere in the Pitakas, of the inclusion of both groups 
under the aggregate of thirty-seven bodhipakkhiya- 
dhamma that we find in the Commentaries and the 
Saddharma-pundarika, and the mention of which in the 
Netti-pakarana went to influence the late Prof. Edmund 
Hardy in assigning the date of that work.” 


— agen

Buddhaghosa, in commenting on the term in the Jhāna- 
vibhaņga of the present work, assumes that all the thirty- 
seven were virtually included by the Buddha, when im- 
parting this portion of the Dhamma, but that only the 
Bojjhanga’s were set forth explicitly, because of their 
special suitability for the practice of lokiya,! as well as 
of lokuttara Jhana—that is to say, of Jhana prac- 
tised with a view to rebirth, as well as of that which was 
cultivated by aspirants to Nirvana (*...niyyānikay 
apacayagamiyn’).? 

It is, however, more probable that, at the time when the 
Mahavagga of the Sanyutta Nikaya, and, perhaps, not very 
long after, the Vibhagga, were compiled, the extension of 
bodhipakkhiyà dhammā as a technical term was 
by no means fixed, but that more time was required for 
the accretion under the common name of those other 
aggregates, the cultivation of which is said to have been 
urged on the brethren by the aged Buddha in his last 
address in the Kūtāgāra-sālā at Vesali. If this be so, 
we should understand the question, in the Sanyutta 
Nikaya and in the Vibhanga :—Katame bodhipakkhiya 
dhammā ?—as meaning not ‘Which are the...’ but 
‘Which are bodhipakkhiya dhamma?’ 

We do indeed find the 87 dhammā aggregated under 
a class-name in the Pitakas, at least in the Abhidhamma 
Pitaka, to wit in the Vibhanga itself (p. 972). But that

as fixed at 37.

class-name is saddhamma. And it is interesting to see 
that, when the Jataka-atthakatha was written, both sa d- 
dhamma and bodhipakkhiya dhamma were 
equivalent class-names for the 87 (Jat. v, 488). 

One more point worthy of notice in this brief foreword 
is that the four ethical exercises, termed in the Dh. S. 
brahmavihārā, are in the Vibhanga called by the 
name of Appammanfiayo. The former term is used 
in the Sutta Pitaka repeatedly—in the Digha and Maj- 
jhima Nikayas, and, for the Metta at least, in the Khud- 
dakapatha—but, so far as I know, the name here used has 
not hitherto been met with in the Tipitaka.! 

The tendency in the Vibhanga—in which the texts all 
show vacillation—to substitute a masculine form in the 
plural of certain leading terms of neuter gender —aya- 
tana, saccā, indriyā, bojjhanga, jhana, 
vi ñ iia na—may also find mention here. 

In the next place the Vibhanga, besides following with 
a fair show of symmetry an arrangement of matter peculiar 
to itself, makes an extended application of that organon 
or vehicle for the cultivation of the moral intellect which 
we find both set out, and to some extent applied, in the 
Dhamma-Sangani. By organon or vehicle I mean, firstly, 
analysis of concrete moral consciousness, as well as of 
rupa as the Stof of consciousness, but in itself unmoral, 
avyakata (Dh. S., $$ 1 foll., and $8 588 foll.) ; secondly, 
the scheme of concentrative discipline of thought entitled 
Jhana. Now, in the Vibhanga, in the opening chapter 
devoted to the five skandhas or constituents of personality, 
we do not find, under ripakkhandha, a repetition of 
the analysis of rupa, as given in the Dhamma-Sangani 
under the more general head of sabban rupay; but 
we do find an inquiry into the properties of the other four 
skandhas. Thus, the two books between them complete 


a E EE — 


! The list constituting the Dharmasarirasutra (see prev. 
page) gives both terms as apparently distinct series. 


Preface xvii 


the logical analysis of the skandhas, as the compilers of 
Abhidhamma envisaged the process. 

That process, be it said by the way, should, for all its 
forbidding appearance, possess no small interest for us, 
in that it resembles our more modern logieal procedure 
known as Determination, or the conjunctive and disjunc- 
tive combination of terms, far more than the older system 
of classification by way of genus, species, and differentia. 
This latter method would, of course, have been repugnant 
to Buddhists, as involving the philosophical principle of 
substance and coinhering qualities, a principle that is 
graphically immortalized in every Porphyrian tree. For 
the Buddhist, things and our ideas of things were not 
analyzable into substance and qualities. They were aggre- 
gates—the interpretations by mano or viññana of 
the various forms of impression or 'contact'—phassa. 
These were analyzable into a number of relations or 
aspects making up the Buddhist view of life and the 
universe. And to understand any given term, or name 
of an aggregate, was to know it in all the relations, under 
all the aspects that were recognised in their philosophy 
and ethics.! 

Next, with regard to Jhana, the Vibhanga again appears 
as extending the methods of the Dhamma-Sangani. The 
prior work, concerned throughout with the investigation of 
things as dhammā, is content to state, for every variety 
of rapt contemplation, that the concrete consciousness at 
the time is analyzable into a number of constant, and a few

1904), describes the procedure as: 'dass alle [dem zu 
erlauternden Begriffe] untergeordneten Spezialbegriffe auf- 
gezáhlt werden, d. h. dass sein Umfang definiert wird.’ I 
would venture to suggest co-ordinated equivalent concepts 
for ‘untergeordneten, etc. Adhivacanani the Buddhist 
would call them. They are by no means exhausted in 
their collective * Umfang ' by that of the term et 


xviii Preface 


varying dhammā. The Vibhaņga, without upsetting 
this aspect of Jhāna, and, for that matter, quoting the 
formula of it now and again, is more concerned to present 
Jhàna, itself a general way of access to meditation, as 
applied to the cultivation of special disciplines in self- 
mastery. It shows how consciousness, as it becomes con- 
centrated by way of Jhana stages, is to be focussed on to a 
given series of concepts taken in order; for example, the 
Satipatthana, Bojjhaņgā, dc. This method is, to a 
limited extent, introduced already in the Dhamma-Sangani 
(pp. 52-55). But in the Vibhaņga it is followed with this 
` difference: The concrete consciousness at the time (tasmiņ 
samaye . . . hoti) is interpreted, not in terms of dhamma 
as such, but as consisting in the given concept or series 
of concepts which is being 'focussed. These are, of 
course, themselves dhammā, but they are taken syn- 
thetically, not analytically. For instance, in the Sacca- 
vibhaņga, where this applied Jhana is first introduced, the 
constant schema of the Dhamma-Saygaņi and its application 
may be seen on p. 112 and pp. 106-111 respectively. 

It may be noted incidentally that, among the various 
forms or schemes of Jhana, the Vibhanga makes use 
of Sufifiata, but not of Appanihita or Animitta (cf. 
Dh. S., §§ 844 foll., 505 foll). And further, that, even in 
the vibhanga devoted to the subject of Jhana, the physical 
side of the exercise is dismissed in the briefest possible 
terms: solitude, the one posture, the, so to speak, local 
concentration of the representative imagination. No men- 
tion of later terms such as kammatthana, appana, 
parikamma, upacara; and only one kasina— 
that of pathavi —alluded to! The one hint we obtain 
that the procedure was akin to that described so circum- 
stantially in later works, like the ‘ Visuddhi Magga,’ 
or the ‘ Yogavacara’s Manual’ (edited by Professor Rhys 
Davids, P.T.S., 1896), is the Atthakatha’s comment: sati 
upatthitā . . . nasikagge va mukhanimitte va (p. 252). 
Buddhaghosa’s Commentary explains this as referring to 


Preface xix 


* the middle portion of the upper lip, where strikes the air 
breathed from the nose. He also refers to further ex- 
planations in the Patisambhida.! 

Finally, as to procedure not found in the prior work, the 
Vibhagga, starting as do the Dathukatha, Puggalapaītatti, 
and Yamaka, with khandhā, dhatuyo, āyatanāni, 
saccāni—that is to say, with the consideration of 
elements or constituents in man, in the universe, and in 
tbe supreme truths about both—goes on to deal with the 
indriyàni;* that is, with man as having faculties, in 
other words, as potent to modify the resultants of those 
combined elements. Next comes the Paccayakara 
(not termed in this work Paticcasamuppāda), the great 
question of the facts of life as conditioned or mutually 
dependent, and then nine groups of ethical culture, 
by which that great network might best be discerned 
and those modifications brought to pass. The next 
book surveys the whole field of ñana — cognition or 
knowledge—from its bases (vatthuni) in the messages 
of sense up to the insight of a tathagata, or perfectly 
wise being. The next investigates all the modes of kilesa 
or corruption by which insight is hindered. And the last 
book, suggesting by its fine title, the Heart of the Dhamma, 
more than it contains, gives a number of summaries and 
a good deal of affirmation, much of it mythological, about 
the conditions of life in this and other spheres—in human 
beings and other beings. | 

The method followed in each book or vibhaņga, with a 
few exceptions, is to set out the category or series form- 
ing the subject in phraseology drawn verbatim, or com- 


— M o —— A ——— MÀ HÀ 


! Now being edited by Mr. Arnold C. Taylor. 

2 Cf. 8. i; 196: ‘So me dhammay adesesi khandhe 
ayatanani dhatuyo ca.’ 

5 The Dh. K. and Pp. follow the Vibhanga here; the Y. 
places the indriyayamaka after the sankhara- 
and anusaya-yamakāni. 


XX Preface 


piled approximately verbatim, from the Nikayas, where 
the treatment makes for edification. Next, the subject 
is analyzed in terms of Abhidhamma—that is, of the 
Dhamma-Sangani—where the intention is analysis with 
a view to mental clearness and consistency, and cultiva- 
tion by way of Jhana. Lastly, the terms of the subject are 
submitted to logical determination with reference to certain 
philosophical ideas. This division is termed Pafihapuc- 
chaka or catechism, and must have remained a tradi- 
tional name of this mode of mental training, since Bud- 
dhaghosa, in his Atthasalini and Sammoha-vinodani, uses 
it without comment. 

We may say, then, that the Vibhanga was conceived as 
a manual for students no less than was the Dhamma- 
Sangani. In it, as in all the seven Abhidhamma books, 
there is no intention on the part of the compilers of setting 
forth their ethical philosophy or psychological ethics in 
any complete and systematic order. Acquaintance with 
the Dhamma is, as I have said elsewhere,' taken for 
granted. The object is not so much to extend knowledge 
as to ensure mutual consistency in the intension of ethical 
notions, and to systematize and formulate the theories 
and practical mechanism of intellectual and moral pro- 
gress scattered throughout the Sutta-pitaka. This, coupled 
with the evident fact of its having been, as tradition 
tells, compiled, not in writing, but for oral teaching 
and for learning by rote (possibly with the help of 
leading terms written down as notes), makes it and all 
Abhidhamma matter so impossible as food for the reader. 
Our own books of symbolic logic and other advanced 
methods of formal logic are not so very possible for him 
either. The Buddhists had no symbols, or used sets of 
words approximately as such. They had attained to the 
consciousness of a wonderful order existing in the sub- 
jective experience of man. Without the stimulus which, 
from one mysterious set of conditions or another, impels 


! Psychological Ethics . . . the Dhamma-Saygant, xxvii. 


Preface xxi 


some races to study the stars and the rest of external 
nature, they turned their growing powers of observation 
and analysis on to the investigation of that inner cosmos. 
Clearness and consistency in the definition or determination 
of ideas was of the first importance, both from the intangible 
nature of their subject-matter, and from their lack of 
tangible or visible registering apparatus. And the only 
possible way in some cases for the burdened memory to 
find its way to and fro was no doubt that endless but 
orderly repetition of a verbal frame-work, wherein, it might 
be, only one term of a series was varied at a time. 

Some day I believe that the Buddhist revivalists will 
take council and compile a version of the Abhidhamma so 
adapted to the economy and other altered conditions of the 
written word as to be interesting and stimulating even to 
alien readers. 

C. A. F. Rays Davips.

ghanasamphassaja cetana jivhāsamphassajā cetanā kāya- 
samphassajā cetanā manosamphassajā cetanā—ime vuc- 
canti saņkhārā atita.

nibbatta apātubhūtā anuppannā asamuppanna anutthita 
asamutthitā anāgatā anāgataņsena sangahita — cakkhu- 
samphassajà cetanā pe . . . manosamphassajā 
cetanā—ime vuccanti saņkhārā anagata.

Tattha katame sankhara olarika? 

Akusala sankhara olarika, kusalavyakata sankhara 
sukhuma; kusalakusala sankhara oļārikā, avyakata san- 
khārā sukhuma. Dukkhaya vedanaya sampayutta sar- 
khara oļārikā, sukhāya ca adukkhamasukhaya ca veda- 
nahi sampayutta sankhara sukhuma; sukhadukkhahi 
vedanahi sampayutta sankhara olarika, adukkhamasuk- 
haya vedanāya sampayuttā sankhara sukhumā. Asama- 
pannassa sankhara oļārikā, samāpannassa saņkhārā suk- 
huma. Sasava sankhara oļārikā, anāsavā saņkhārā 
sukhuma. 

Te te và pana sapkhare upaday’ upādāya sankhara 
oļārikā sukhumā datthabbā. 

Tattha katame sankhara hina?! 

Akusala sankhara hina, kusalāvyākatā sankhara panita; 
kusalākusalā sankhara hina, avyākatā sankhara panita. 
Dukkhaya vedanaya sampayutta saņkhārā hina, sukhāya 
ca adukkhamasukhaya ca vedanahi sampayuttā sankbara 
panita; sukhadukkhahi vedanahi sampayutta sankhara 
hina, adukkhamasukhaya vedanaya sampayutta sankhara 
panita. Asamapannassa sankhara hina, samapannassa 
saykhārā panita.  Sasava sankhara hina, anāsavā say- 
khārā paņītā. 

Te te và pana saņkhāre upaday’ upadaya sankhara hina 
panita datthabbā.

Dasa dhātuyo na vattabbā sukhaya vedanāya sampa- 
yutta ti pi dukkhaya vedanaya sampayutta ti pi aduk- 
khamasukhaya vedanāya sampayutta ti pi.  Paūca 
dhātuyo adukkhamasukhaya vedanaya sampayutta. Ka- 
yavififianadhatu siya sukhaya vedanāya sampayutta siya 
dukkhāya vedanāya sampayutta. §Manovifinanadhatu

naya sampayutta siya adukkhamasukhaya vedanāya sam- 
payutta. Dhammadhatu siya sukhaya vedanāya sampa- 
yutta siya dukkhaya vedanaya sampayutta siya adukkha- 
masukhāya vedanāya sampayutta siya na vattabba su- 
khāya vedanaya sampayutta ti pi dukkhaya vedanāya 
sampayuttà ti pi adukkhamasukhaya vedanaya sampa- 
yutta ti pi.

Katame dhamma akusala ? 

Yasmin samaye akusalay cittan uppannay hoti somanas- 
sasahagatan ditthigatasampayuttan, rüpárammanar vā? 
saddarammanan va gandhārammaņay và rasarammanar

phasso, phassapaccaya vedanā, vedanāpaccayā tāņhā, 
taņhāpaccayā upādānay, upādānapaccayā bhavo, bhava- 
paccaya jati, jātipaccayā jarāmaraņay. Evam etassa ke- 
valassa dukkhakkhandhassa samudayo hoti.

moho akusalamūlay: ayan vuccati avijja.

Ya cetanā saūcetanā sayņcetayitattay $ ayan vuccati 
avijjāpaccayā sankharo. 

Tattha kataman sankharapaccaya viññanan ? 

Yan cittay mano maànasar hadayay pandaran mano 
manayatanay manindriyay viūūāņay viūūāņakkhandho 
tajjā manovinüanadhátu: idan vuccati sankharapaccaya 
vinñanan.

Vedanakkhandho saññakkhandho sankharakkhandho: 
idan vuccati viññanapaceaya naman.

manāyatanay manindriyay viūūāņay viññanakkhandho 
tajjà manovinnanadhàtu: iday vuccati nāmapaccayā chat- 
thāyatanay.

vuccati chatthayatanapaccaya phasso.

228 Satta bojjhaņgā 


Tattha katamo satisambojjhango ? 

Atthi ajjhattay dhammesu sati, atthi bahiddhā dham- 
mesu sati. Yada pi ajjhattay dhammesu sati, tadā pi sati- 
sambojjhaygo abhiniiaya sambodhaya nibbānāya sayvattati 
Yada pi bahiddhā dhammesu sati, tada pi satisamboj- 
jhaygay abhiünnaya sambodhaya nibbanaya saņvattati. 

Tattha katamo dhammavicayasambojjhango ? 

Atthi ajjhattay dhammesu pavicayo, atthi bahiddha 
dhammesu pavicayo. Yada pi ajjhattay dhammesu parvi- 
cayo, tada pi dhammavicayasambojjhaņgo abhiiinaya sam- 
bodhaya nibbānāya saņvattati. Yada pi bahiddha dham- 
mesu pavicayo, tada pi dhammavicayasambojjhango a- 
bhiünaya sambodhaya nibbanaya sanvattati. 

Tattha katamo viriyasambojjhango ? 

Atthi kayikar viriyay, atthi cetasikar viriyay. Yada pi 
kāyikay viriyay, tada pi viriyasambojjhaggo abhifinaya 
sambodhaya nibbanaya sayvattati. Yada pi cetasikan 
viriyay, tada pi viriyasambojjhaggo abhiññaya sambo- 
dhaya nibbanaya sanvattati. 

Tattha katamo pitisambojjhango ? 

Atthi savitakka-savicara piti, atthi avitakka-avicara pīti. 
Yada pi savitakka-savicara-piti, tada pi pitisambojjhango 
abhinüaya sambodhaya nibbānāya sanvattati. Yada pi 
avitakka-avicara piti, Lada pi pitisambojjhango abhinnaya 
sambodhāya nibbanaya sanvattati. 

Tattha katamo passaddhisambojjhango ? 

Atthi kayappassaddhi, atthi cittappassaddhi. Yada pi 
kayappassaddhi, tadà pi passaddhisambojjhaņgo abhinnaya 
sambodhaya nibbanaya sanvattati. Yada pi cittappas- 
saddhi, tada pi passaddhisambojjhaggo abhiünüaya sam- 
bodhaya nibbanaya sanvattati. 

Tattha katamo samadhisambojjhango ? 

Atthi savitakka-savicaro samadhi, atthi avitakka-avicaro 
samadhi. Yada pi savitakka-savicaro samadhi, tada pi 
samadhisambojjhaggo abhinünaya sambodhaya nibbānāya 
sanvattati. Yada pi avitakka-avicaro samadhi, tada pi 
samadhisambojjhango abhinnaya sambodhaya nibbānāya 
sanvattati. 
 Tattha katamo upekhasambojjhango ? 

Atthi 8jjhattag dhammesu upekha, atthi bahiddha 
dhammesu upekhā. Yada pi ajjhattay dhammesu upekha, 
tada pi upekhasambojjhango abhiūiiāya sambodhāya nib- 
banaya sanvattati. Yada pi bahiddha dhammesu upekha, 
tada pi upekhasambojjhango abhiünaya sambodhaya nib- 
banaya saņvattati.

Ariyo atthangiko maggo, seyyathīday: sammaditthi 
sammasajkappo sammāvācā sammakammanto sammi- 
ajivo sammāvāyāmo sammāsati sammasamadhi. 

Tattha katama sammāditthi? 

Dukkhe iāņay dukkhasamudaye iiāņay dukkhanirodlie 
Donan dukkhanirodhagāminiyā patipadāya ina: ayan 
vuccati sammāditthi. 

Tattha katamo sammasarkappo ? 

Nekkhammasagkappo, avyapadasankappo, avihiņsā- 
sankappo: ayar vuccati sammasarkappo. 

Tattha katama sammāvācā ? 

Musavada veramani,  pisunàya vacaya veramani, 
pharusaya vacaya veramani, samphappalapa veramani: 
ayar vuccati sammavaca. 

Tattha katamo sammakammanto ? 
micchacara veramani: aya vuccati sammakammanto. 

Tattha katamo sammā-ājīvo ? | 

Idha ariyasavako miccha-ajivan pahaya samma-ajivena 
jīvitay kappeti: ayan vuccati sammā-ājīvo. 

Tattha katamo sammāvāyāmo ? 

Idha bhikkhu anuppannānay pāpakānay akusalanan 
dhammānay anuppadaya chandan janeti vāyamāti viriyay* 
ārabhati cittan paggaņhāti padahati, uppannānay pāpa- 
kanan akusalanan dhammānay pahanaya® . . . pe... 
anuppannanar kusalanan dhammanay uppadaya . 
uppannānay kusalānay dhammānay thitiya asammosāya 
janeti vāyamati viriyay  arabhati cittay pagganhati 
padahati : ayar vuccati sammavayamo.

avyákatà . . . pe... kati sarana kati arana?

vedanāya sampayutto ; satta magganga siya sukhaya 
vedanāya sampayutta siya adukkhamasukhaya vedanaya 
sampayutta. Siya vipaka siya vipakadhammadhamma. 
Apupādiņņā ; anupādāniyā; asankilittha-asankilesika.

siya savitakka-savicārā siya avitakka-vicaramatta siya 
avitakka-avicara. Sammasankappo pitisahagato, sukha- 
sahagato, na  upekhasahagato; satta magganga siya 
pitisahagata siya sukhasahagata siya upekhasahagata.

dassanena na bhavanaya pahatabbahetuka.

gàmino. Siya sekha siya asekha. Appamana; appa- 
māņārammaņā. Panita. Siya sammattaniyata siya 
aniyata. Na maggarammana; siya maggahetuka siya 
maggadhipatino siya na vattabba maggahetukā ti pi 
maggadhipatino ti pi.

bhāveti, viviec' eva kamehi . . . pe . . . pathamay 
jhānay  upasampajja  viharati pathavikasinar,* tasmir 
samaye paūcaņgikay jhānay hoti: vitakko vicaro piti 
sukhay cittassa ekaggatā. Idan vuccati pathamar jhānay. 
Avasesa dhamma jhānasampayuttā.* 

Tattha kataman dutiyan jhānay ?

bhaveti, vitakkavicārānay vūpasamā ... pe... dutiyay 
jbānay upasampajja viharati  pathavikasinar, tasmiy 
samaye tivaņgikay jhānay hoti: piti sukhay cittassa 
ekaggata. Idan vuccati dutiyay jhanay. Avasesa dhammā 
jhānasampayuttā.

272 Catasso appamaiifidyo 


XIII. 
APPAMANNAVIBHANGO. 


CaTTasso appamaünayo: idha bhikkhu mettāsahagatena 
cetasā ekar disar pharitvā viharati, tatha dutiyan tatha 
tatiyar tatha catutthan, iti uddham adho tiriyay sabbadhi 
sabbattataya sabbāvantay lokan mettasahagatena cetasā 
vipulena mahaggatena appamanena averena avyapajjhena 
pharitvā viharati; karunasahagatena cetasa ekan disay 
pharitva viharati, tatha dutiyan tatha tatiyar tatha catut- 
than, iti uddham adho tiriyay sabbadhi sabbattataya 
sabbāvantay lokan karunasahagatena cetasa vipulena 
mahaggatena appamanena averena avyapajjhena pharitva 
viharati ; muditāsahagatena cetasā ekan disan pharitvā 
viharati tatha dutiyar tatha tatiyan tathā catutthan, iti 
uddham adho tiriyan sabbadhi sabbattataya sabbavantay 
lokan muditāsahagatena cetasā vipulena mahaggatena

pavatteti. 


Katamāni dasa ? 

Idha Tathāgato thanaü ca thanato atthanaü ca at- 
thanato yathabhutan pajanati, yam pi Tathāgato thánaün 
ca thanato atthanan ca atthanato yathābhūtan pajanati, 
idam pi tathāgatassa tathāgatabalay hoti, yay balan 
agamma Tathāgato āsabhatthānay patijānāti parisāsu 
sihanaday nadati brahmacakkay pavatteti. 

Puna ca paran Tathagato atitanagatapaccuppan- 
nanan kammasamadananay thanaso hetuso vipakar yath- 
abhütar pajànati, yam pi Tathagato atitanagatapaccup- 
pannānay kammasamādānānay thanaso hetuso vipakay 
yathābhūtay pajānāti, idam pi tathāgatassa tathagata- 
balan hoti, yay balan agamma Tathāgato asabhatthanan 
patijanati parisāsu sihanadan nadati brahmacakkan 
pavatteti. 

Puna ca paray Tathagato sabbatthagāminiy  pati- 
paday yathābhūtay pajanati, yam pi Tathāgato sabbat- 
thagāminiy patipadan yathabhutan pajanati, idam pi 
tathagatassa tathagatabalan  hoti, yan balay āgamma 
Tathāgato āsabhatthānay patijānāti parisasu sīhanāday 
nadati brahmacakka: pavatteti. 

Puna ca paran Tathāgato anekadhatun nānādhātuy 
lokan yathābhūtay pajanati, yam pi Tathagato aneka- 
dhātuy nānādhātuy lokay yathābhūtay pajanati, idam pi 
tathāgatassa tathagatabalan hoti, yan balan agamma 
Tathāgato āsabhatthānay patijanati parisāsu sihanadan 
nadati brahmacakkay pavatteti. 

Puna ca paran Tathāgato sattanan nanadhimuttikatan 
yathābhūtay pajanati, yam pi Tathagato sattānay nana-

dhimuttikatay yathabhutayn pajānati, idam pi tathagatassa

asabhatthànar patijanati parisāsu sīhanāday nadati brah- 
macakka: pavatteti. 

Puna ca paran Tathāgato parasattānay parapuggal- 
lanan indriyaparopariyattay yathabhütar pajanati, yam pi

Katame te sattā mahārajakkhā ? 

Dasa? kilesavatthūni : lobho doso moho māno ditthi vici- 
kiccha thīnay uddhaccay ahirikay anottappar.  Yesay 
sattānay imani dasa kilesavatthūni āsevitāni bhāvitāni 
bahulīkatāni ussadagatāni: ime te sattā mahārajakkhā. 

Katame te sattā apparajakkhā ? 

Yesar sattānay imani dasa kilesavatthūni anāsevitāni 
abhāvitāni abahulīkatāni anussadagatāni: ime te sattā 
apparajakkhā. 

Katame te sattā mudindriyā ? 

Paūcindriyāni : saddhindriyay viriyindriyay satindriyay 
samādhindriyay paūūindriyay.  Yesay sattanan imani 
paūcindriyāni anāsevitāni abhāvitāni abahulīkatāni anus- 
sadagatāni : ime te sattā mudindriyā. 

Katame te sattā tikkhindriyā ? 

Yesay sattānay imāni paūcindriyāni āsevitāni bhāvitāni 
bahulīkatāni ussadagatani: ime te sattā tikkhindriya. 

Katame te sattā dvākārā ? | 

Ye te sattā pāpāsayā pāpānusayā pāpacaritā papadhi- 
= mahārajakkhā mudindriyā: ime te satta dva- 

ara. 

Katame te satta svakara ? 

. Ye te satta kalyāņāsayā kalyānacaritā kalyanadhimut- 
tikā apparajakkha tikkhindriyā: ime te satta svakara. 

Katame te satta duvifiinapaya ?* 

Ye va te satta dvākārā te va te satta duviūūāpayā. 
Yeva te sattā svākārā te va te satta suvififiapaya. 

Katame te sattā abhabbā ? 

Ye te sattā kammāvaraņena samannāgatā kilesāvaraņena 
samannāgatā vipākāvaraņena samannāgatā assaddha ac- 
chandikà duppañña abhabba niyaman okkamitur kusalesu 
dhammesu sammattan: ime te satta abhabba.

Tattha katama indriyesu aguttadvāratā ? 

Idh' ekacco cakkhunā rūpay disvā nimittaggāhī hoti 
anuvyalijanaggāhī, yatvadhikaranam enay cakkhundriyay 
asanvutan viharantan abhijjhādomanassā papaka akusalā 
dhammā anvassaveyyuy, tassa saņvarāya na patipajjati 
na rakkhati cakkhundriyan cakkhundriye na sanvaray 
āpajjati, sotena sadday sutvā,* ghanena gandhay ghayitva, 
jivhaya rasan sāyitvā, kāyena photthabbay phusitvā, man- 
asa dhammay viūiiāya nimittaggahi hoti anuvyaūjanag- 
gahi, yatvadhikaranam ensy manindriyay asanvutay 
viharantay abhijjhādomanassa pāpakā akusalā dhammā 
anvāssaveyyur, tassa sanvaraya na patipajjati na rak- 
khati manindriyan manindriye na sanvarayn apajjati: ya 
imesay channar indriyānay agutti agopanā anarakkho 
asanvaro: aya vuccati indriyesu aguttadvāratā.

amattannuta.

nadhimuccati na sampasidati, anagatan va addhānay

dati, etarahi vā? paccuppannan addhānay arabbha 
kaņkhati vicikicchati nadhimuccati na sampasidati. 

Imani tini tamāni.

evaņditthī:* yan kiūcāyay purisapuggalo  patisarvedeti 
sukhan va dukkhan và adukkhamasukhan va sabban tan 
pubbe katahetuti.

issaranimmanahetuti.

Tattha katame tayo kiūcanā ?

Tattha katamani tini anganani? 
Rago anganan doso anganan moho anganan. Imani tini

Tattha katamāni tini malāni ? 
Rago malan doso malan moho malay. Imani tini 
malāni. 


Tattha katamāni tīņi visamani? 

Rago visamay doso visamar moho visamar. Imani tini 
visamāni. 

Tattha katamāni aparani pi tini visamāni ? 

Kāyavisamay vacīvisamay manovisamay. Imani tini 
visamāni. 

Tattha katame tayo aggī ? 

Rāgaggi dosaggi mohaggi. 

Ime tayo aggī. 


Tattha katame tayo kasāvā ? 

Rāgakasāvo dosakasāvo mohokasāvo. Ime tayo kasāvā. 
Tattha katame apare pi tayo kasāvā ? 

Kāyakasāvo vacīkasāvo manokasāvo. Ime tayo kasāvā. 


Tattha katamā assādaditthi ? 

Idb’ ekacco samano và brahmano và evaņvādī hoti 
evanditthi: natthi kamesu doso ti, so kāmesu patavyatay 
apajjati. Ayan vuccati assādaditthi. 

Tattha katama attanuditthi ?!

massa akovido ariyadhamme avinito sappurisanar adassāvī 
sappurisadhammassa akovido sappurisadhamme avinīto, 
rupan attato samanupassati, rüpavantar va attanan, attani 
va rupan, rüpasmir và attānay; vedanan... saiifian...

vantan và attānan, attani va vilüànar, vinnanasmir và 
attanar: ya evarūpāditthi ditthigatan ... pe. . . vipari- 
yesagaho: ayan vuccati attanuditthi.

dhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyaya. Idan 
catutthan kusitavatthu. 

Puna ca paran bhikkhu gama: va nigamay và piņdāya 
varanto na labhati lūkhassa va panitassa va bhojanassa 
yavadatthan pāripūriy. Tassa evan hoti: ahan kho 
gamal và nigamar va pindaya caranto nalatthan lükhassa 
và panitassa và bhojanassa yavadatthan paripurin, tassa 
me kayo kilanto akammaīto. Handāhay nipajjàmiti. So 
nipajjati na viriyay àrabhati appattassa pattiya anadhiga- 
tassa adhigamaya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyaya. Idan 
paficamay kusitavatthu. 

Puna ca paran bhikkhu gamar và nigamar và pindaya 
caranto labhati lukhassa và panitassa và bhojanassa yāvad. 
atthar paripurin. Tassa evan hoti: aba kho gaman và 
nigamay và pindaya caranto alatthar lukhassa và panitassa 
và bhojanassa yavadatthar paripurin, tassa me kayo 
garuko* akammajifio masacitan manne. Handahan nipaj- 
jāmīti. So nipajjati na viriyay ārabhati appattassa pattiya 
anadhigatassa adhigamaya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyaya. 
Idan chatthar kusitavatthu. | 

Puna ca paran bhikkhuno uppanno hoti appamattako 
abadho. Tassa evan hoti: uppanno kho me ayan appam- 
mattako abadho, atthi kappo nipajjitug. Handāhay nipaj- 
jàmiti. So nipajjati na viriyar arabhati appattassa pattiya 
anadhigatassa adhigamaya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyaya. 
Idan sattama: kusitavatthu. 

Puna ca parar bhikkhu gilanà vutthito hoti aciravutthito 
gelanna. Tassa evan hoti: ahan kho gilānā vutthito 
aciravutthito gelañña, tassa me kayo dubbalo akammanñno. 
Handáhar nipajjāmīti. So nipajjati na viriyag arabhati 
appattassa patiya anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchika- 
tassa sacchikiriyaya. Idan atthaman kusitavatthu. 

Imani attha kusitavatthuni.

Katame 421 


Kamavacara dhammā, na kāmāvacarā dhammā; rupa- 
vacara dhammā, na rūpāvacarā dhammā; arupavacara 
dhammā, na arūpāvacarā dhammā ; pariyāpannā dhammā, 
apariyapanna dhammā. 

Katame dhammā kāmāvacarā ? 

Hetthato' avīcinirayay pariyantay karitvā uparito 
paranimmitavasavattideve? anto karitva yan) etasmiy 
antare etthavacara ettha  pariyapannà khandhadhatu- 
āyatanā* rupan“ vedanā sañña sankhara viññanan: ime 
dhamma kamavacara. 

Katame dhamma na kamavacara ? 

Rūpāvacarā arūpāvacarā apariyāpannā: ime dhammā 
na kāmāvacarā. 

Katame dhammā rūpāvacarā ? 

Hetthato  brahmalokar pariyantan karitva uparito 
akanitthe deve anto karitvā yan etasmiy antare ettha- 
vacara ettha pariyāpannā samapannassa va? uppannassa 
va ditthadhammasukhaviharissa® va cittacetasika 
dhamma: ime dhamma rupavacara. 

Katame dhamma na riipavacara ? 

Kāmāvacarā arūpāvacarā apariyipanna: ime dhammā 
na rupavacara. 

Katame dhammā arūpāvacarā ? 

Hetthato akasanaficayatanupage’ deve puriyantay karitva 
uparito neva-saiifia-nasaifayatanupage deve anto karitva 
yay etasmiy antare etth’ avacara ettha pariyapanna samā- 
pannassa và uppannassa và ditthadhammasukhavihārissa* 
và cittacetasikā dhammā: ime dhammā arūpāvacarā. 

Katame dhammā na arūpāvacarā ? 

Kāmāvacarā rūpāvacarā apariyapanna: ime dhammā 
na arupavacara. 

Katame dhamma pariyapanna? 

Sasavà kusalākusalāvyākatā dhammā kāmāvacarā rūpā- 
vacarā arūpāvacarā: rüpakkhandho vedanakkhandho sañ- 
ūākkhandho saņkhārakkhandho viūūāņakkhandho: ime 
dhammā pariyāpannā. 

Katame dhammā apariyāpannā ? 

Maggā ca maggaphalāni ca asankhata ca? dhatu: ime 
dhamma apariyāpannā.

diņņupādāniyay. Cattarayatana siya upadinnupadaniya 
siya anupādiņņupādāniyā. Dve ayatana' siya upādiņņu- 
padaniya siya anupadinnupadaniya siya anupadinna- 
anupadaniya. 

Dasa dhatuyo upādiņņupādāniyā. Saddadhatu anupā- 
diņņupādāniyā. Paūca dhatuyo upadinnupadaniya siya 
anupadinnupadaniya. Dve dhatuyo siya upādiņņupā- 
dāniyā siya anupadinnupadaniya siya anupādiņņa-anupā- 
daniya. 

Samudayasaccan anupadinnupadaniyay. Dve sacca 
anupadinna-anupadaniya. Dukkhasaccay siya upādiņņu- 
padaniyay siya anupadinnupadaniyay. 

Navindriyā upādiņņupādāniyā. Domanassindriyay anu- 
pādiņņupādāniyay. Tinindriya anupādiņņa-anupādānijā. 
Navindriya siya upādiņņupādānijā siya anupadinnupa- 
daniya siya anupadinna-anupadaniya. ` 

Tayo akusalahetu anupadinnupadaniya. Tayo kusala- 
hetu siya anupadinnupadaniya siyā anupadinna-anupa- 
daniya. Tayo avyakatahetu siya upadinnupadaniya siya 
anupadinnupadaniya siya anupadinna-anupadaniya. 

Kabalinkaro aharo siya upadinnupadaniyo siya anupa- 
dinnupadaniyo. Tayo ahara siya upadinnupadaniya siya 
anupadinnupadaniya siya anupadinna-anupadaniya.
